The Lantern Ledger

Chapter 1 Harbor

The children racing past the fountain treated the day as if it belonged entirely to them, and Arin envied that innocence while measuring how much of an old fear that returned with the cold had already seeped into their games. Because every roof answered the weather in its own language, the route to the river quarter never felt like a straight line, and Arin suspected the city preferred it that way. The talk in the clockmaker lane turned on prices and weather, yet beneath it all ran the older question of how anyone could continue to mend a family promise broken in winter while facing an old fear that returned with the cold.

If the council wanted peace, it wanted the ornamental kind, the version that kept ledgers tidy while leaving ordinary people to negotiate with an old fear that returned with the cold alone. Each page in the unlikely journal suggested that someone before them had tried to mend a family promise broken in winter, failed quietly, and left behind enough detail for a more stubborn reader to continue. Chapter 2 Lantern
